# Part 3 evidence ledger, survey batch (Claude Code session)

**Date:** 2026-09-30 · **Session:** Claude Code (Part 3 research, in parallel with the Codex session) · **Scope:** the evidence behind `notes/part3/2026-09-30-self-improvement-survey-mining-zh.md`, the reading of the survey *Self-Improvements in Modern Agentic Systems* (arXiv 2607.13104v1) and of the works on its project page and in its bibliography. New IDs continue the repository maxima E668 and D445 (E644–E668 and D436–D445 were added by the Codex session on 2026-09-30): E669–E1313 and D446–D594 here.

## How the evidence was produced

The rule of 2026-09-29 (a source is evidence only if its full text was read and each statement is locked to a specific sentence or table row) and the scripts in `tools/evidence-check/` were used unchanged; the steps differ from the 2026-09-29 ledger only in the search.

1. **Candidates.** The survey's project page (312 entries: 77 that change model parameters, 176 that change the scaffold, 59 evaluation entries) and the works cited in the survey's §6, §7.1, §7.2, §7.6, §8 and §9 that the page does not list (109 entries): 421 candidates. Each was screened from its arXiv abstract (or the page's own summary when no abstract was available) for what it changes, what signal it uses, whether it learns from the agent's own trajectories, and whether it reports any time or cost. Edwin's instruction: works that change model parameters are read at abstract level only.
2. **Read.** 64 works were read in full for the first time (records s001–s064; s000 is the survey itself, 40 items), and 23 works already in the verified store were reused (records h, w, n, x, g). One reader per work saved the full text and wrote a record of up to 16 items, each with the claim, the figure as printed, the measurement definition, the location, the line number in the saved text and a verbatim anchor.
3. **Mechanical check.** A script checked that each anchor occurs at its line and that the item's numbers stand within three lines of it.
4. **Audit.** A second reader, who did not write the record, tried to refute each item against the saved text; 1,063 items were kept, none dropped, and corrected items appear with the corrected wording.
5. **Source rule.** Each work was screened under the citation priority Edwin restated on 2026-09-30 (top venues, then preprints from top universities or established laboratories, then official material of OpenAI/Anthropic-level companies; Findings and workshop papers count by institution). Of the 65 new records, 27 are top-venue, 33 institutional supplements, 4 held and 1 excluded.
6. **Use.** Only items that passed steps 3 and 4, from works that passed step 5, are cited. The document was checked by script (every number against the cited items) and by three independent reviewers (evidence, intent, readability).

**Counts.** 65 new records (s000–s064; 60 used, 4 held, 1 excluded), 1,063 audited items of which 983 are in the records file; 23 earlier records reused (h, w, n, x, g batches; their items are in the 2026-09-29 records files). The document cites 91 records (645 items) after aliases; E669–E1313 below are those 645 items in order of first appearance. Section 2 holds the 149 D-entries that survived the audit of 157 drafted (140 confirmed, 9 corrected by the auditor, 8 rejected).

**Quotations.** As on 2026-09-29, this file and the records file give the location of every item and no verbatim text of the sources; the anchors stay in the verified store outside the repository (decision for Edwin, section 4).

**Not done.** No experiment was reproduced. No budget was computed. The slides, the canonical dossier and the Codex files were not changed; the notes files were moved into `notes/part1`, `notes/part2` and `notes/part3` at Edwin's request, with the references updated.

### 1c Works with more than one record

No two s-records describe the same work. Where a work on the survey's list already had an audited record from an earlier batch (h, w, n, x or g), that record was reused and no new s-record was written; those works and their duplicate records are listed in section 1c of `research/2026-09-29-part3-evidence-ledger.md`.

### 1d Works found by the sweeps and not verified

Not applicable to this batch: the candidates came from the survey's project page and bibliography, not from finder sweeps. Entries screened by abstract and not read are listed in appendix C of the document, and the 421 screening records are in the store.

## 2 D-ledger additions

The entries below were drafted from the records of this batch and then audited one by one against both places they compare (the ledger line or the first place in the source, and the second place in the source). [A] = conflict between the ledger and a source; [B] = conflict inside a source; [C] = version or venue note. Where the auditor corrected the resolution, the corrected text is shown. Replacement wording for ledger lines is in the Resolution column.

| ID | Work | The ledger or the source says (place A) | The source says (place B; location, version) | Resolution | Source (read 2026-09-30) | Items | Audit | Source class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D446 | [B] Self-Improvements in Modern Agentic Systems: A Survey (s000) | §4.2, Eq. 8 (l.463-467): scaffold updates give fast, reversible, task-specific adaptation without the risk of catastrophic forgetting. §8.1 (l.1968, l.1984): b_t is the cumulative resource budget, the cost of the improvement pipeline; m_t is the capability score. | §3.3 (l.434): moving adaptation into the scaffold does not remove forgetting and adds its own failure modes (memory poisoning, drift in tool semantics, template dependence). l.1992 calls b_t the agent's execution budget. §3, §6.2 and the Notation table (l.4950-4952) use m_t for the memory component; uppercase Φ is the data filter in §5.1 (l.543-544, l.5071-5073) and the evaluator in §8.1 (l.1973). v1. | Do not cite the survey for "no forgetting" from scaffold updates; cite §3.3's qualified statement. ### 3a Replacement text for existing lines

No existing ledger line is replaced by this batch: section 2 records only conflicts inside sources ([B]) and version or venue notes ([C]). The canonical dossier is not changed.

Entries E669–E1313. Each is one audited item of the verified store; the P3 record column gives the item, whose full record is in the records file of its batch.

| E669 | **SICA ranks agents by a utility (Eq. 1): U = w_score*p_score + w_cost*(1 - min(1, p_cost/$10)) + w_time*(1 - min(1, p_time/300 s)), where U is the base utility, p_score in [0,1] is the normalised benchmark performance score, p_time is the wall-clock time in seconds, p_cost is the dollar cost, and w_score, w_cost, w_time are weights set to 0.5, 0.25 and 0.25; $10 per problem is the cap on the worst cost and 300 s is the timeout after which the agent is cancelled (lines 184-196). Eq. 2: U_final = U(1 - tau) if the run timed out, otherwise U, with timeout penalty tau = 0.5, to give partial credit for work done before the timeout (lines 197-203). Algorithm 1 takes evaluation benchmarks B = {b_j : j = 1..m} and an iteration count n and outputs the improved agent system A_n (lines 275-288).** (w_score 0.5, w_cost 0.25, w_time 0.25; cost cap $10 per problem; timeout 300 s) | Objective definition. Half the weight is benchmark score and half is efficiency: cost and wall-clock time enter as linear credit for staying under the $10 and 300 s caps. The weights are the authors' settings, not measurements. | https://arxiv.org/html/2504.15228v2, arXiv 2504.15228v2 (HTML full text saved as .txt); PDF v2 t…; §3 Methods, Eq. 1-2; line 193 of the saved text | 2025-05-16 | Primary, preprint | P3 record s063#1 | audited: supported |
| E670 | **Write admission is formalized per candidate memory item m as a paired A/B comparison. Eq. 2: o_A = F(I_A), o_B = F(I_B), where F is the model execution inside the SCEC (self-contained execution context). Eq. 3: DeltaR = R(o_B) - R(o_A), DeltaL = L(o_B) - L(o_A), DeltaT = T(o_B) - T(o_A), where R is the task-specific reward, L latency and T token overhead. Eq. 4: composite admission score S = DeltaR - lambda_L*DeltaL - lambda_T*DeltaT, with lambda_L, lambda_T >= 0 controlling the trade-offs between utility and cost. | E703 | **Judge pitfalls: when Phi_judge produces m_t, the protocol should disclose the judge model version, prompt, rubric kappa and the evidence shown to it, and should isolate the judging budget from the agent's execution budget b_t, because judge reliability varies with its resources (context limits, multi-agent debates, tool-use steps) and unreported judge overhead hides whether the agent improved or the evaluator became more exhaustive (line 1992). | E766 | **There are two gates. (i) From generation sd onward (Algorithm 2, 'if i >= sd', line 815), a generated candidate p' is evaluated only if E[M_k(p')] + Var[M_k(p')] + E[error_k] >= hyper_M x max(D.score()) (Eq. 3, lines 109-112). The first two terms are the mean and variance of the five models' predicted scores, E[error_k] is their average test error, and max(D.score()) is the best score found so far; adding variance and error keeps the filter conservative. For each parent, generation continues until n candidates pass or 3n attempts are made (Algorithm 2, line 823). Before generation sd, all n candidates generated per parent are evaluated without filtering (Algorithm 2, lines 839-843). (ii) The top k prompts by score in D become parents for the next level (Algorithm 1, line 798), and the output is the highest-scoring prompt (line 804). Settings: n = 20 kid prompts per parent in the first level and 8 afterwards; k = 5; hyper_M = 0.8; sd = 4. Search stops when the last three levels bring no score improvement. All methods explore the same number of candidates (line 448).** (n = 20 (first level) / 8 (later levels); k = 5; hyper_M = 0.8; sd = 4) | Hyperparameters of the search and of the score-model filter; the acceptance criterion is the task progress score averaged over trials; not a result. | https://arxiv.org/html/2402.08702v4, arXiv 2402.08702v4 (HTML full text saved as .txt); PDF v4 t…; §4.3 Experimental Setups, 'Hyperparameters'; §3.2 Eq. 3; Appendix B Algorithms 1-2; line 448 of the saved text | 2024-10-03 | Primary, published: EMNLP 2024, main conference: Proceedings of the 2024 Conference on Empirical Methods in N… | P3 record s018#6 | audited: corrected by the auditor, corrected wording shown |

| E887 | **Credit assignment over the agent's own history: for each new memory MemQ records parents(m_new) = A, the retrieved set, which induces a provenance DAG (line 147). For task s_j with retrieved set A_j, reward R_j and new memory m_new,j, the per-memory TD error is delta(m_i, j) = R_j + gamma Q(m_new,j) - Q(m_i) for every m_i in A_j (Eq. 5, line 243); gamma = 0 reduces this to MemRL-style single-step EMA (line 246). A backward BFS from each retrieved memory m_0 adds alpha * (gamma*lambda)^{d(m, m_0)} * delta(m_0, j) to every reachable ancestor m, with d the shortest DAG path length (Eq. 6, lines 248-253). The BFS stops at maximum depth D (D = 4 in Table 3, lines 939-941) or when (gamma*lambda)^d < 10^-12; increments are buffered within an epoch with update counts N_i and flushed at epoch end as Q(m_i) += clip(DeltaQ(m_i)/N_i, -eps_clip, eps_clip) (lines 253-257).** (discount floor (gamma*lambda)^d < 10^-12) | Update rule; the numbers are algorithm settings, not measurements. | https://arxiv.org/html/2605.08374v3, arXiv 2605.08374v3 (HTML full text saved as .txt; body line…; §4.3 Provenance-Based Credit Assignment, Eqs. 5-6; Algorithm 1 lines 21-28; line 253 of the saved text | 2026-05-14 | Primary, preprint | P3 record s001#4 | audited: supported |

| E892 | **Six benchmarks, each split into a training set (for memory accumulation) and a held-out test set: LifeLongAgentBench (multi-step OS-level agent planning), BFCL v3 (multi-turn function calling, graded by backend state comparisons against ground truth), LiveCodeBench v6 (competitive code generation), MMMU Pro (10-choice multimodal reasoning), ERQA (multimodal, physically grounded question answering) and GPQA Diamond (graduate-level science QA). All methods use frozen backbones: GPT-4o-mini for LLAB, Qwen3.5-35B-A3B for BFCL, Gemma-4-E4B-it for the other four. | E941 | **The component that changes is the student agent's tool library, which sits outside the model. | E1019 | **The paper formalizes a training-free execute-reflect-reuse loop rather than an optimization objective. The agent faces a stream of tasks q^(1), q^(2), ... and keeps a memory M that starts empty (M_0 = empty set). For task q^(k), entries are retrieved from the current memory M_(k-1), usually by similarity-based vector search and LLM-based filtering, and put into the interaction context. The frozen LLM policy pi then acts as a_t ~ pi(. / c_t; M_(k-1)), where c_t is the interaction context at step t (c_1 holds the query q and a system prompt), a_t is the action (a code block or a tool call), o_t is the environment observation and c_(t+1) = c_t (+) (a_t, o_t) (line 100). The rollout stops at step T (a terminal action or a step budget) and yields the trajectory tau^(k) = (q, (a_1,o_1), ..., (a_T,o_T)) (line 101). One or more reflector modules then write back M_k = Reflect(M_(k-1), tau^(k)). Metis instantiates this as S_i = Select(q_i, M_(i-1)), tau_i ~ pi(. / q_i, S_i), M_i = Reflect(M_(i-1), tau_i) (lines 139-144). No single objective combining accuracy and cost is written; §4.1 reports accuracy, execution cost and memory-construction cost as separate metrics (lines 278-281).** | Formal problem statement (loop definition); not a measurement. | https://arxiv.org/html/2606.24151v1, arXiv 2606.24151v1 (HTML full text saved as .txt; only vers…; §2.2 Self-Evolving Agent Systems (with §2.1 and §3.2 'Memory Schema'); line 104 of the saved text | 2026-06-23 | Primary, preprint | P3 record s003#0 | audited: supported |
| E1020 | **The objective is stated as a design principle rather than an optimisation formula. The paper's core view is that agent performance depends on how much decision-relevant information is introduced, maintained and updated within a limited context budget (line 143). It frames context engineering as a constrained optimisation that trades completeness against conciseness, with naturalness as a supporting constraint (line 174), and GA targets a budget of under 30k tokens (line 193). The only formula for the budget is Eq. 1 (lines 272-287): C_H = sum over messages m in the conversation history H of len(m), where len(m) is the character length of message m after JSON serialisation; B = alpha * W_tokens, where W_tokens is the configured token window and alpha ~ 3 chars/token is an empirical ratio that turns the token budget into a character budget. Compression or eviction is triggered when C_H > B. The authors note that alpha = 3 causes mildly early eviction for ASCII text (about 4 characters per token) and risks delayed eviction and context overflow for CJK text (1-2 tokens per character). The saved text gives no formula for 'information density' itself.** (alpha ~ 3 chars/token) | Problem statement and context-budget trigger rule; not a measurement. | https://arxiv.org/html/2604.17091v1, arXiv 2604.17091v1 (HTML full text saved as .txt; PDF v1 te…; §1 Introduction; §2.1 Design Principles; §2.1.4; §2.3.4 Eq. 1; line 272 of the saved text | 2026-04-18 | Primary, preprint | P3 record s027#0 | audited: supported |

Expansion policy: the parent is drawn by Thompson sampling from Beta(tau(1 + n^C_success(a)), tau(1 + n^C_failure(a))) over all agents, i.e. on clade counts (line 180; lines 618-624). Evaluation policy: the agent to test on one more task is drawn by Thompson sampling on its own counts, Beta(tau(1 + n_success(a)), tau(1 + n_failure(a))), among agents with remaining tasks (line 185; line 634). The authors say that decoupling expansion from evaluation, with every evaluation at the granularity of a single agent-task pair, enables early stopping on unpromising agents (line 109). tau is a scheduler increasing with time, favouring exploration early and a polarized distribution near the end (line 178), set to B/b with b the remaining budget; alpha = 0.6 (line 207). | E1247 | **The authors list four limitations of autonomous exploration (lines 453-461). The 30-round execution cap means highly complex research tasks may span multiple sessions, with continuity kept only through written reports and task-list annotations. The reflection-based weight adjustment is a preliminary design and has not yet accumulated enough long-term data to definitively prove its effectiveness across diverse, real-world user workflows. The self-improvement log relies on manual user curation. Advanced skill-tree maintenance (merging categories, deprecating tools, restructuring) remains entirely manual. Other stated caveats: the Discussion's findings are distilled from practice rather than derived from controlled ablations alone (lines 1002-1003); the alpha = 3 heuristic risks delayed eviction and context overflow on CJK text (lines 284-287); efficiency ratios are comparable only within a benchmark block (line 463); and validation of the full evolutionary path (skill consolidation, autonomous exploration and architectural self-update) is left as an open problem (lines 1085-1088).** | Authors' stated limitations; not a measurement. | https://arxiv.org/html/2604.17091v1, arXiv 2604.17091v1 (HTML full text saved as .txt; PDF v1 te…; §3.3 'Limitations'; §5 Discussion; §2.3.4; Table 2 caption; line 457 of the saved text | 2026-04-18 | Primary, preprint | P3 record s027#15 | audited: corrected by the auditor, corrected wording shown |

## 4 Still open

- **Decision for Edwin: quotations.** Locations only (as here), or verbatim quotes as the repository rule asks.
- **Decision for Edwin: the survey itself.** It is a preprint (OpenReview lists it as under review at TMLR), so under the source rule it is an institutional supplement. The document uses it as the source of the formal framework and as the index of works; its descriptions of other works are treated as secondary and are not cited as evidence for those works.
- **Decision for Edwin: Findings and workshop papers.** They are counted by institution, as on 2026-09-29. Held under this reading: SAGE (EMNLP 2026 Industry Track), Learning to Evolve (Findings of ACL 2026), MUSE-Autoskill and SIA (preprints without an eligible institution); excluded: RoboPhD. The Codex review uses MUSE-Autoskill's time and token figures; the document holds it (its section 9.4).
- **Not verified: the works the Codex review cites from outside the survey.** GLoW (arXiv 2509.24116) is cited by the Codex review, is not on the survey's list and has no record in the store; A-Mem and Alita are on the list, were screened by abstract only (relevance 1) and were not read; of the twelve evaluation benchmarks the Codex review cites, AppWorld has an audited record (s054) and the other eleven (WorkArena, WebArena, ST-WebAgentBench, DiscoveryWorld, PhysGym, Mind2Web, WebLINX, MINT, OSWorld, ClawBench, AstaBench) were screened by abstract only. The document's section 9 lists them as a separate verification task. SpeedRunner, ActionEngine, WALT, HarnessFix, StarHarness and ASI are not on the survey's list but have audited records in the 2026-09-29 ledger.
- **Abstract-only works.** The 77 parameter-changing entries and the entries screened out by abstract are listed in the document's appendix C with an abstract summary and a relevance score (the full screening fields are in the store's screening file); none is used as evidence. Of the survey's 443 references, 109 became candidates and about 175 are already on the project page (calc., matched by title and arXiv id); the remaining about 159 were not screened.
- **Unresolved inside sources.** The [B] entries of section 2 record numbers that disagree inside one paper (for example CODESKILL's step counts, which are averaged over different task sets before and after). They are cited, if at all, with that caveat.
- **Figures in images.** Values shown only in a figure are not in the saved texts and were not used, except where an item says the value was read from a caption or the text (PROMST's corrected-score plots are an example of values that exist only as figures).
- **Talk transcript.** Edwin may send the transcript of a talk; the Codex review used its subtitles for concepts. Nothing from it is in this batch.
- **Merging.** E-entries and D-entries of this file are proposed records for v3.1. The canonical v3 is unchanged.
